- **Almost all of it does come back character for character.** A plain save over the same file with nothing touched writes every recorded place back with the text it already held — checked two ways at once, by asking the running Blender and by pulling the path text straight out of the file's bytes, the two agreeing. That covers a plain relative place, a full path, a `..` segment that still resolves, a doubled separator, the wrong letter case, a name in characters outside the Latin alphabet, a name with a space in it, a trailing space, a place pointing at nothing, a place one folder up, a template with its braces still in it, and the places recorded for a library, a font, a sound, a volume, a text, an OSL script node, a light-profile node, the bake picture, the cloth cache, the geometry-nodes bake folder and the sequencer's proxy folder.

- **The first thing that changes is the separator, and which way follows the operating system rather than the Blender version. The re-spelling happens when the file is *opened*, and the plain save then writes it into the file.** On the Creator's Mac and on the rented Linux machine, a place written with a backslash comes back written with a forward slash. **On the Windows machine it runs the other way and is much larger: every forward slash in a recorded place becomes a backslash.** Nothing else about the text moves in either direction — the leading `//` stays, a doubled separator stays doubled, a `..` step stays, letter case stays, a trailing space stays. **Seven recorded places keep their forward slashes on Windows and are the only ones that do** — the compositor output node's folder, the cloth cache folder, the geometry-nodes bake folder, the bake picture's place, the sequencer's proxy folder, the linked library's own place and the weak reference beside it — **the last two only because both are `//lib.blend`, with no separator in them to change**, so five real places are spared. **The render output path is not among them**, and it is itself a place written to, so *places written to are left alone* is not the rule. All four versions do the same thing on each machine.

- **The second is that a data-block nobody is using is dropped at the save, taking its recorded place with it.** An image with no users and no fake user disappeared at the plain save in every version on all three machines, and the weak reference beside it — recording the library it had been appended from — went too. A fake user preserved it.

- **The consequence the comparison states in its own words, and it is the one M8 turns on: a check that compares whole files, or their checksums, says nothing about whether a recorded place moved — the separator re-spelling and the dropped data-block both move the bytes. Only the path text can be compared.**

- **The two ways of listing a file's recorded places.** `bpy.utils.blend_paths` and `bpy.data.file_path_map` **agree with each other about spelling**, so far as three macOS runs settle it: two found them agreeing in the process that built the file, one of those comparing the strings character by character by script, and the third found them disagreeing — that run's own stored output reads the other two's way. **What changes the spelling is the reload, not the route.** On the Windows machine the two agree in all four versions, in the building process and after a reload. **Whether they ever disagree about spelling is carried as not established.** **They do return different collections, which is a different thing from spelling** — 24 strings against 27 on the Mac in every version, the extras being repeated copies of places both report; the same on Windows with larger totals, and with the linked library's own inner picture among the extras, because one route reaches through the library and the other does not.

- **Saving somewhere else is the other reading of the question and answers differently, so the boundary is load-bearing.** Saving into a **different folder** rewrites and tidies every relative place a file is *read* from and leaves the places it *writes* to untouched; saving into the same folder under a new name changes nothing, nor does Save Incremental, nor does a plain save with the remap setting deliberately switched on. **On Windows, saving onto a second drive turns every relative place into a full path naming the old drive and says nothing about it**, and Make Paths Relative across drives names the place it could not form, counts the failure and **still reports itself finished**.

**What is bounded:** every Windows result is a Server edition, no desktop edition of Windows having been reached; a cache file, a movie clip and a movie strip were never in the test file, so what a save records for their places is unasked; a place on a network share written the way Windows writes one is untested; and whether saving leaves a linked library untouched was run in 5.2 alone.

**Whether the failure this entry was numbered for occurred: in kind, yes.** **A plain save does re-spell a recorded place the artist never touched**, so the shape M4 and M8 are exposed to is real rather than theoretical. **What fires it is a recorded place whose separator is not the opening machine's own** — a Windows-authored file opened on Mac or Linux, and on Windows every file that arrived from anywhere else, which is the wider half. The dropped unused data-block is a second shape and removes a place outright rather than re-spelling it. **What it costs M4 and M8 is the runner's to read, and nothing is moved here**: both must be read against this answer, and **whichever of the two listing routes the check walks has to be named** rather than left to whoever builds it, the two routes returning different collections.

*(The original entry's remainder follows, unchanged, since what it says breaks is what the answer bears on.)*

**What breaks without it, and this is the reason it was worth a number:** if an ordinary save re-spells references, **every remap pair of every re-uploaded file misses at once**, every one is reported *not applied*, the render finishes, and it reports success. That is the silent wrong render `Dependency Upload & Remap` exists to prevent, in the one document `Method` singles out for that failure shape.

**What holds without it:** the marker, and a check re-run.

**What rests on it:** `Dependency Upload & Remap` **§12 item 22** and **M8**'s narrow *reference re-pointed* reading.

### Fact 129 — What each of Mac, Windows and Linux permits a process that is being ended to run before it is gone: which kinds of ending give it any chance to run code at all, what a write started at that moment is guaranteed to reach disk, and which endings give nothing.

**Named by:** `Audits/Architecture/Sweep/Reporting a problem.md` Fact A, 2026-08-25, out of the row for what Smoozy manages to say on its way down — **Row 456**. `Preparation/Specs/Reporting a problem.md` carries an **unresolved** label against it in its own words, and `Preparation/Groundwork/Open Threads.md` names it as one of two things that spec hands this stage. **The label was checked rather than trusted**: nothing in `Preparation/Architecture/` answers it — `State, identity and recovery` D6 states the opposite arrangement rather than this one, and `Desktop app and delivery` §21's service list names no such capability.

**A force-quit and the machine running out of memory are named specifically**, because the promise rests on those two being indistinguishable from each other.

**Answered by:** reading each operating system's own documentation, and tried on a computer running each. **No rental and no rented machine** — Smoozy does not run on the rented machine at all. **Read the publications and not only the manual pages**, which is `Preparation/Architecture/Method.md`'s standing note and has closed five facts in this register already.

**Machine:** none that can be rented. It wants a computer of each of the three systems Smoozy runs on. The Creator has an Apple-silicon Mac; **no Windows machine and no Linux desktop computer has run it** — `Desktop app and delivery` Y3, and `Open Threads`' *"**Due:** when a Windows computer exists. Nothing schedules it."* **So the Windows third is documentation-only by necessity rather than by choice**, and closes as unresolved unless a machine appears, exactly as `Desktop app and delivery` §1.3 already requires of every such clause.

**Shape-deciding:** no. **Posable:** the documentation half now, on all three; the trial on the Mac now and on nothing else.

**ANSWERED, 2026-08-25, on all three — and one of the three is observed rather than read.** See `The three desktop systems read again — dying, revealing, resolving, and what the shell does not expose.md` §1. **Which endings give the process a chance:** the catchable ones do and the uncatchable ones give nothing whatever, on all three. **The write is not safe until it is forced down** — a buffered write inside a handler was reproduced twice producing a **zero-byte file** while the handler had demonstrably run, so the last line is a raw write followed by each platform's own full-sync call. **Force-quit on the Mac is catchable**, observed on the Creator's own machine — but at a stated width: the programming interface was exercised, the Force Quit panel was not, and never against an application that had stopped responding. **The spec's own ceiling is unmoved:** a force-quit and the machine running out of memory stay indistinguishable from each other and from any other death. Windows and Linux are documentation alone, this project having neither computer.

**What breaks without it:** the diary's on-the-way-down line — what Smoozy manages to write as it is being ended, and whether a force-quit and an out-of-memory kill can be told apart at all.

**What holds without it, and it is why this closes rather than escalates:** the spec already states its own ceiling, so a negative narrows the catch rather than breaking the promise. Row 456 closes unresolved with the promise standing.

**What rests on it:** Row 456.

**A neighbour, not the same question — Fact 130.** This one is about what can be recorded on the way down; that one is about what can be read on the way back up, and an answer to either does not settle the other. The `Whole app` re-sweep states the distinction in those words.

### Fact 130 — Whether a desktop application can tell, at its next launch, that the previous run did not end cleanly, on each of Mac, Windows and Linux.

**Named by:** `Audits/Architecture/Sweep/Whole app — the reporting additions re-sweep.md`, the one fact that sweep names, 2026-08-25 — *"whether a quit the user asks for always reaches the application before it ends, and what each of a system restart, a forced logout, a power loss, a force-quit, and the machine running out of memory presents as at the next launch"*. Wanted by **Row 467**, the launch-time crash row.

**Reached from the other side by `Audits/Architecture/Sweep/Design — Whole app — Design close re-sweep.md`**, whose **Row 479** — the box at the next launch that says nothing was lost — records that it waits on nothing outside, because what it meets first is `State, identity and recovery` **D6**: *"No durable state depends on the desktop application shutting down cleanly. Every durable write happens at the moment the fact becomes true, and nothing is written on exit. A crash and a clean close therefore leave the same durable state, and there is no recovery path for one that is not the ordinary path for the other."* **The mechanism the spec proposes — a mark written at the moment the user asks to quit — is a durable write on exit, which is the one thing D6 says nothing does.** So the fact and D6 are two separate obstacles in front of the same promise, and answering the fact does not on its own close either row.

**Answered by:** reading each operating system's own documentation, and tried on a computer running each. **No rental and no rented machine.**

**Machine:** none that can be rented — the same three-computer shape as Fact 129, and **the Windows third cannot be posed today** for the same reason, so it closes as answered for two systems and unresolved for the third.

**Shape-deciding:** no; the sweep names it as a fact its row needs and claims no arrangement turns on it. **Posable:** the documentation half now; the Mac trial now.

**ANSWERED, 2026-08-25, on all three, and the answer is the same on all three: the application must keep its own mark.** See `The three desktop systems read again — dying, revealing, resolving, and what the shell does not expose.md` §2. **One negative in the reading was false and its own citation check caught it** — Apple ships a framework that reports how previous runs ended, unelevated and in-process, so *nothing exists* was wrong. **The advice survives whole**, because that framework still cannot see a force-quit. Windows' recovery facility fires only on an unhandled fault or a hang, wants the user's consent and has a floor of a minute; Linux publishes nothing for it at all. Windows and Linux are documentation alone.

**What breaks without it:** the promise is *after a run of Smoozy that crashed, Smoozy says so* — an arrangement that cannot separate the two says so after an ending that was not a crash, or stays silent after one that was, and either way the sentence on the page is not what the user meets.

**What rests on it:** Rows 467 and 479, and through Row 467 the exception `Preparation/Specs/Whole app.md` grants to its own failure rules.

### Fact 131 — Whether a Blender crash file's backtrace section or Python backtrace section carries values out of the machine's own environment.

**Named by:** `Audits/Architecture/Sweep/Reporting a problem.md` Fact B, 2026-08-25, out of **Row 463**. `Preparation/Groundwork/Open Threads.md` names it as the second of the two things `Preparation/Specs/Reporting a problem.md` hands this stage. The spec's **unresolved** label carries a standing instruction rather than a hedge: *"Until that is answered the crash file is not taken."*

**Most of the crash file is already settled by the research, and this entry states what so that nobody pays to learn it twice.** The sweep went by the fixed route — `Preparation/Blender Research/Across topics/The map.md`, then `When a render ends.md`'s *Whether a crash file is written* section, then the one comparison question it names, read for that question alone. What is established:

- *"A Blender made to crash writes a crash file and says so, at 139, in all four versions: on Linux in the temporary folder under the loaded file's name, on the Mac in that machine's own temporary folder."*
- The file *"held a version line, a backtrace section and a Python backtrace section"*, and on Linux it is named after the blend-file rather than `blender.crash.txt`, which is the only name the manual gives.
- *"Anything printed immediately before the crash never reaches the ordinary output."* For that one ending the crash file is the only place those words exist at all, which is what the promise reaches for.
- **The file is written for one kind of ending only.** *"Running the machine's own memory out writes no crash file at all, at either of the two statuses that ending gives"*, and whether one is written when a Blender is replaced under a running render is **not settled** — two runs of one test on one machine disagree and both stand.
- Windows is untried, which does not reach this promise: the machines Smoozy renders on run Linux.

**What none of it answers is the label's own question** — whether either backtrace section carries environment values. The three sections are named and their contents are not described. **The residue is exactly one question and this entry does not widen it.**

**Not a Blender Research question, and it must not be sent as one.** The sweep rules it a reading of a produced file: the research has been consulted and does not answer it, and `Preparation/Blender Research/` is not to be opened again for it.

**ANSWERED 2026-08-25, on the Pile 3 rental, and the answer is negative — the crash file carries no environment values at all.** The answer of record is [The Pile 3 batch](The%20Pile%203%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20a%20full%20mount%20under%20a%20running%20supervisor,%20an%20interrupted%20heavy%20frame,%20and%20an%20hour%20of%20silence.md) §5, on Blender 4.5.3 on a rented Ubuntu 22.04 container. Two unmistakable marker values were placed in the environment — one shaped like a licence key, one like a private path — and Blender was crashed twice, once with no scene and once with a real scene open. **Neither marker appears anywhere in either file, and neither file contains assignment-shaped text of any kind.** Both are 1775 bytes and hold three sections and nothing else: a version header; a `# backtrace` of module paths and hex addresses, every path either the Blender installation's own or a system library's; and a `# Python backtrace` of source paths and line numbers. Label: **observed in a test**.

**One thing the reading adds, and it is about the file's name rather than its contents.** With a scene open Blender wrote `heavy.crash.txt`, and a search inside the file for that scene's name found nothing — **so the contents never name the user's file and the name always does.** That confirms the research's own *named after the blend-file* on a second machine, and it is the only user-derived string anywhere in the arrangement.

**What follows for the documents.** `Reporting a problem` G5's exclusion was written against the possibility of environment values and there are none, so **the crash file may be taken** and **Row 463 closes answered rather than unresolved**. `Security and licence` §13 rule 4's bar on the licence key and the computer identity is unaffected — it never reached the crash file for those, and nothing found here puts either in one.

**Machine:** answered on the Pile 3 rental. **It rode free exactly as this entry said it would**: nothing was rented for it and nothing was spent on it beyond the batch's own 93 minutes.

**Shape-deciding:** no. **Posable:** yes, on the next rental of any kind.

*(The block below is the record of this entry **before** Fact 131 was answered on 2026-08-25, kept because it states the rule the question was posed against. **It is not this entry's status** — the status is the ANSWERED paragraph above, and the answer is no. A live `NOT ANSWERED.` line stood here beneath that paragraph until 2026-08-29, so the register said both things at once and the half nearest the bottom contradicted `Reporting a problem` G5, which takes the file. Found by that document's stage-close gate, which reads this register for its labels.)*

**What was written here before the answer.** `Preparation/Architecture/Security and licence.md` §13 rule 2: *"the machine's environment is never captured into any record Smoozy keeps or shows"*, on the observed ground that a rental's own settings hold secrets in plain text. A crash file taken whole and attached to a mail the user sends is precisely that act if the answer is yes.

**What breaks without it:** nothing silently. An unanswered fact closes Row 463 as unresolved with the crash file excluded and the promise standing, which is what the promise already instructs.

**What rests on it:** Row 463.

### Fact 132 — What each of Mac, Windows and Linux offers an application for showing a folder, or a named file inside its folder, in that system's own file manager; whether the chosen shell exposes it; and whether it survives each of the three build outputs.

**THE SHELL CHANGED UNDER THIS ENTRY, 2026-09-02, AND THIS ONE IS OWED A READING. What is read below is Wails v2's facility, and the selected shell is Tauri v2** — `Desktop app and delivery` H5b was filled by Build and Tauri came out materially smaller. **The entry stands as a reading of Wails and is not struck**, because a dated reading is not falsified by a later choice. **The three operating systems' own halves are unaffected** — they are readings of Mac, Windows and Linux and no shell choice touches them. **What is owed is the shell half alone: one reading of the selected shell's own publication on whether it exposes the operation**, wanting no machine and no rental. **When:** at the step that consumes it, which is the reveal's, not before. **What must not be done:** assume the selected shell exposes it because the one read here did.

**This is the one of the twelve to answer first, and not because it is hard.** Four sweep agents reached it independently on 2026-08-25, from four different documents, and it is owed in at least four places at once. **One answer closes all four.**

**Named by, four sweeps:**
- `Audits/Architecture/Sweep/Reporting a problem.md` Fact C — *"What each of Mac, Windows and Linux offers an application for opening a folder with one named file shown in it"*. Says outright that it also closes the gap `Preparation/Architecture/Download.md` H7 already has, so one answer serves two promises.
- `Audits/Architecture/Sweep/Design — Whole app — Design close re-sweep.md` — *"what each of Mac, Windows and Linux offers for showing a named file in the user's file manager, and whether Wails exposes it"*. Adds the shell half, and notes that Linux is the same shape as its downloads folder and its notification service: a desktop convention rather than a guarantee.
- `Audits/Architecture/Sweep/Design — Transfers — Design close re-sweep.md` — *"what each of the three operating systems offers an application for showing a folder to the user in that system's own file manager, and whether that route survives the packaging each build uses."* **Adds the build half**, which is why it is in the question above: `Desktop app and delivery` §19.1 builds three different things — a notarized macOS `.app`, a Windows side-by-side install, a Linux package — **and the answer may differ across them.**
- `Audits/Architecture/Sweep/Design — the mockup — Design close re-sweep.md` — found *Files on your computer* drawn three times in the mockup and the report box's saved reading saying the folder *"Smoozy has just opened"*, searched every file in `Preparation/Architecture/` for *reveal*, *file manager*, *open the folder*, *Finder*, *Explorer* and *xdg-open* and found **nothing**, and filed no row of its own because the promise belongs to documents other sweeps hold. It recorded it as *"the shape of thing that falls between three sweeps"*, which is exactly what happened.

**The four places it is owed:**
- **Row 465** — `Preparation/Specs/Reporting a problem.md`'s central act: Smoozy *"writes a single file, offers a place to put it, opens the folder it landed in so the file is in front of them"*.
- **Row 477** — the same act in `Preparation/Design/Whole app.md`: *"The folder has opened behind the box, so the file is in front of the user rather than somewhere they must go and find."*
- **Row 500** — `Preparation/Design/Transfers.md`: *"A finished download reads Files on your computer"*.
- **`Preparation/Architecture/Download.md` H7, which has carried the gap since it was written** — *"What the *show a job's downloaded files* control is handed is the set, never a folder. One place, and it opens."* That is `Preparation/Specs/Queue & History.md`'s *show a job's downloaded files*, whose own words are *"This opens the local folder that job's outputs landed in"*. **Row 311 carries what the control is handed, not how it opens**, and H8 requires an unreachable place to be *"presented as a place Smoozy cannot open rather than dropped from the set"* — a distinction that only exists if opening is a thing that can be attempted and can fail, and nothing anywhere says what attempts it.

**Why it is a fact rather than a detail.** `Preparation/Architecture/Desktop app and delivery.md` **A1**: *"A feature module reaches the operating system only through a named service of this document, and never directly"*, and *"A module that opens a file, posts a notification, reads a name, draws a menu or opens a picker for itself has broken this boundary, and the failure is invisible from inside that module."* **§21's list of services is closed and holds no service of this kind** — the platform file adapter, the downloads folder, the secret adapter, the identity adapter, the budgets, the window frame, the notification service, the suppression table, the presentation plumbing, the typography point, the colour scheme, the two faces, the verbatim route, the menus and the system file picker, the local record, the updater. **A picker asks the user for a path; it does not put a folder on their screen.** So the middle verb of four promises has no route on any of the three systems, and the shell's ability to perform it is a property of the chosen shell rather than of a platform — the same shape as H6a's title bar and §18.3's menu bar.

**Merge note.** `Reporting a problem`'s Fact C asked a second half the runner's statement of this fact does not carry: **whether each system's file picker can be run in a save-a-new-file mode where the application supplies the name and the user picks only the folder.** It turns on one clause of that promise — Smoozy *"writes a single file, offers a place to put it"* — and the two readings need different platform calls. It is named here so it is not lost and **stays with Row 465** rather than being folded in silently.

**Answered by:** reading each operating system's own documentation and the chosen shell's, and tried on a computer running each. **No rental and no rented machine** — this is the user's own computer, the same three systems `Desktop app and delivery` §6 and §18 are already written against.

**Machine:** a Windows machine can be rented and a Linux desktop cannot — the three-computer shape of Fact 129, with the Windows third **documentation-only because no Windows machine has been rented for it**, and the Linux desktop third documentation-only by necessity (`Desktop app and delivery` Y3). **Every Windows machine this project has read reported a Server edition**, so a desktop-only part of it is still out of reach of what has been rented.

**Shape-deciding: yes, and the sweep that raised it first says so outright.** If a system offers no reveal-in-folder act, the central sentence of the promise — the folder opened so the file is in front of the user — **cannot be kept on that system**, and what replaces it is the user hunting for a file, which is the one outcome the Creator's stated requirement exists to prevent.

**Posable:** yes, as documentation, now.

**ANSWERED, 2026-08-25, on all three — and the Linux half is the reverse of what the reading first found.** See `The three desktop systems read again — dying, revealing, resolving, and what the shell does not expose.md` §3. **Mac and Windows both publish a call that opens the folder with the file selected, and both report failure to the caller.** **Linux does too**: the desktop portal publishes an open-the-containing-folder call that selects the file and falls back by itself where nothing owns the file-manager name — the reading had recorded no published route and its citation check overturned that. **Three things a builder must not lose.** The Mac's call **selects a symbolic link rather than what it points at**, so links are resolved first; the variant of it that returns nothing must be refused, because failure has to reach the caller; and Windows fails outright unless its component system is initialised. **The chosen shell reaches none of it** — it refuses `file://` addresses by name and its open-a-thing call returns nothing at all — so this is platform code beside the shell. **The save-a-new-file half is yes on all three**, and the shell exposes it with a default filename. Windows and Linux are documentation alone.

**What rests on it:** Rows 465, 477 and 500; `Preparation/Architecture/Download.md` H7 and H8, and through them Row 311's control; and — wherever the answer lands as a service — `Desktop app and delivery` §21's closed list, which is the only place a module may reach the operating system from.

### Fact 133 — What Telegram requires of a bot's display name and of a bot's username — the permitted characters, the length bounds and the terminal `bot` requirement — and whether anything other than the bot-making chat can tell a candidate username from a taken one.

**Named by:** `Audits/Architecture/Sweep/Design — the mockup — the three drawn modules.md`, 2026-08-25, out of **Row 470** — the first rows this project has filed for `Telegram`'s design, that module having had no design to file against until 2026-08-23.

**It is not a Blender fact and it must not be answered from memory.** It is owed against Telegram's own published bot documentation, which is the same publication `Preparation/Architecture/Telegram.md` §3 B13, §11 I3 and §12 M8 are already read against.

**Answered by:** reading that publication. **No rental and no machine.** **Shape-deciding:** no. **Posable:** yes, now.

**ANSWERED, 2026-08-25.** See `The three desktop systems read again — dying, revealing, resolving, and what the shell does not expose.md` §4. **The username rules are published as a real rule, three times over:** 5 to 32 characters including the required `bot` ending, Latin letters, digits and underscores, not case-sensitive, and **never changeable afterwards**. **The display name has a 64-character ceiling and no published character rule anywhere** — a negative its citation check attacked on a page the reading never opened, and confirmed. **The important negative: nothing available to a bot can tell the user a username is free.** A route exists that answers *occupied or not*, but *may this be used for a new bot* is answered only by a method for user accounts. **And Telegram publishes a ceiling on how many bots one account may own**, which a paid tier raises — a constraint on a plan that asks every user to make their own bot.

**What breaks without it:** the drawing fixes the display name as the literal string *Smoozy* for every user's bot and fixes the username's composition as a stem, four characters and a `_bot` suffix, and nothing in `Preparation/Specs/Telegram.md` or `Preparation/Architecture/Telegram.md` states either. **A candidate Telegram will not accept turns the spec's *minute of work* into a loop the walkthrough cannot detect**, because Smoozy never speaks to the bot-making chat and only learns of a refusal when the user comes back without a token. And the candidate offered again on a tap has to differ from the last one **in a way that changes the collision**, which is a property of the composition rule rather than of the tap.

**Not Fact 69, and the two are easy to confuse.** Row 256 waits on Fact 69, and `Telegram` §21 item 3 states Fact 69's remainder exactly — *"what the bot-making chat does when a unique name is already taken. **Breaks:** one walkthrough sentence's wording — how the collision is described to the user; the mechanism (Smoozy generates another candidate on a tap) needs nothing from the chat."* **That is the wording of the collision message. The composition of the candidate itself is named nowhere.**

**What rests on it:** Row 470, and `Telegram` §6 S1, which says who generates the names and says nothing about what is generated.

### Fact 134 — Whether a `.blend`'s scene records values for Simplify's switch, its maximum subdivision, its child-particle proportion and its texture limit; whether the reading `Render Settings` performs returns them; and which of the supported versions carry which of those settings. **Blender's.**

**Named by:** `Audits/Architecture/Sweep/Design — the mockup — the three drawn modules.md`, 2026-08-25, out of **Row 472**. It is a **Blender question and belongs to Blender Research rather than here** — recorded in this register only so the row that waits on it has a number to name, in the same position as Facts 104, 127 and 128.

**The fixed route was walked and came up short, which is the point of recording it.** `Preparation/Blender Research/Across topics/The map.md` **poses no question for Simplify at all.** The one joined answer that reaches it is `Preparation/Blender Research/Across topics/What can be set from outside.md`, which lists Simplify among the settings with no launch argument in any version. **That settles how a value is imposed and settles nothing about what the file already records or what a read returns.** Carrying it across would be the assembling `Preparation/Architecture/Method.md` forbids. **So this is a new question rather than an unlooked-up one**, and whoever opens Blender Research next should treat it as one.

**A second Blender claim rides on the same fact.** The mockup's Design notes exclude one setting on a version claim — *"Texture Resolution, which only one of the four supported Blenders has and would appear and disappear with the Blender chosen"* — and nothing in the map or the joined answers carries that either.

**Answered by:** the Blender Research route, **asked blind**. **No rental and no machine** — it is a local Blender question. **Shape-deciding:** no. **Posable:** now.

~~**NOT ANSWERED.**~~ **ANSWERED 2026-08-29**, by the blind Blender Research round the Creator directed on 2026-08-28, at `Preparation/Blender Research/Comparison/What a render demands.md` **question 6** — registered blind, answered by four version-isolated passes, audited, hand-tested twice, citation-checked over 177 claims, compared and comparison-checked. **The round rented two Linux machines, together under seven cents, and destroyed both.**

**The answer, in the terms the row needs it.** A saved scene **does** record Simplify's switch and a **pair** of values for each of the three settings — one used for the viewport and one used for the final render — with the texture pair belonging to Cycles and reached by its own path, present whatever the active engine. Every value is readable back with no window open by the documented route, survives saving unchanged, is held **per scene** rather than per file, and survives a file crossing between operating systems. **The verdict is *it differs*, in one thing only: how many values a scene records** — seven in 4.5, 5.0 and 5.1, nine in 5.2, which alone adds a texture resolution scale. **And one thing a reader must not assume is stated outright: an untouched scene does not read back at the documented defaults.**

**So the second Blender claim riding on this fact is confirmed too** — Texture Resolution is 5.2's alone, stated as new in that version's own release notes, and the older three, each asked, answered that the render-side property does not exist.

**What it unblocked:** **Row 472, answered the same day** at `Preparation/Architecture/Rendering.md` §6.1 A1d with the page's half in `Render Settings`. **The drawn premise it was filed on falls** — Simplify is not the one thing Smoozy adds rather than takes out of the file — and the three Rule 10 send-backs are at `Audits/Architecture/Row 472 — what goes back to Design.md`.

**What it did not take with it:** **Fact 135**, which that round deliberately did not carry, so **Row 492 stays blocked**. And Windows on either kind of processor, an exact Linux distribution package, and reading these values without Blender at all remain untried — the round's own stated silences.

*(The original entry's remainder follows, unchanged, since what it says breaks is what the answer settled.)*

**What breaks without it:** the drawing puts three concrete values on the screen — `2`, `0.50`, `1024` — and declares in its own notes that *"there is no file value behind them"* and nothing for a cross to hand back. **If the file does record Simplify values, that premise is wrong**: Simplify is not the one thing Smoozy adds rather than takes out of the file, the italic-and-bold convention the whole panel is built on applies to those three rows like every other, and the drawing's own reason for drawing them plainly is gone. **If it does not, the three values are Smoozy's own defaults and need a home nobody has given them.**

**What holds without it:** Row 22's promise that the three are offered, answered at `Preparation/Architecture/Rendering.md` L10 — *"Simplify's switch and its three settings — Max Subdivision, Child Particles, Texture Limit — as settings pairs, exactly where the payload carries them"*, that clause naming itself *"Row 22 whole"* — with the inferred half carried at that document's §9 item 11. Neither the row nor the answer reaches what the values are or where they start from.

**What rests on it:** Row 472.

### Fact 135 — At what engine and sample count a scene reached through a compositor's Render Layers node is rendered, and whether a value imposed at launch against the job's scene reaches it. **Blender's.**

**Named by:** `Audits/Architecture/Sweep/Design — Render Settings — Design close re-sweep.md`, 2026-08-25, out of **Row 492**. It is a **Blender question and belongs to Blender Research rather than here** — recorded in this register only so the row that waits on it has a number to name, in the same position as Facts 104, 127, 128 and 134.

**The fixed route was walked and came up short.** `Preparation/Blender Research/Across topics/The map.md` routes the compositor to its own questions and **poses no question about a reached scene's render settings**. The joined answers reach the edge of it and stop: `What a file can be asked before it renders.md` records that *"A compositor node reading a second scene renders that scene fresh but does not run its compositor"* — so the second scene **is** rendered — and `What can be set from outside.md` records that *"Naming a scene picks that scene, and its own settings and its own compositor are what run"*, **which is about the scene named at launch and not about one pulled in behind it.** Carrying the second sentence onto the first is exactly the assembling `Preparation/Architecture/Method.md` forbids. **So this is a new question rather than an unlooked-up one**, and the sweep says in as many words that it did not assemble an answer.

**The architecture side is silent too.** `Preparation/Architecture/Render Settings.md` §5 **C1**'s imposition set sets *"the chosen engine's own sample count"*, **C7** imposes the percentage *"against the scene's own percentage setting"* and **D3** assigns *"the scene's own device setting"* — **none of the three names which scene the pair is assigned against**, and the line on the page is only true if it is the job's scene alone. A search of `Preparation/Architecture/` for the pulled-in scene returns `Render Settings` §1.1 Q2 item 3 and `Job files and outputs` §4.2 item 3 and nothing else; neither says anything about the settings such a scene renders at.

**Answered by:** the Blender Research route, **asked blind**. **No rental and no machine** — it is a local Blender question. **Shape-deciding:** no. **Posable:** now.

~~**NOT ANSWERED.**~~ **ANSWERED 2026-08-29**, by a blind Blender Research round run the same day the Creator green-lit the run to Build. All seven steps: registered blind as **The compositor 9**, answered by four blind passes, audited four times, answered again by four fresh blind passes when every audit sent back a gap in the record of looking, hand-tested, citation-checked over 324 claims, compared and comparison-checked. **The answer is at question 9 of `Preparation/Blender Research/Comparison/The compositor.md`. No machine was rented and nothing was spent.**

**The answer, in the terms the row needs it.** A scene reached through another scene's compositor renders with **its own engine and its own sample count**, in all four versions — established by **bit-identical pixels** and by Blender's own log lines, forty comparisons giving forty exact zeros. A **view-layer sample override reaches through the node**, and the **pulled-in scene's own** policy governs it: with opposite policies on the two scenes the count follows the pulled-in scene's, never the compositing scene's. **And only one render happens** — the compositing scene's own view layer is never rendered, because nothing in its tree asks for it.

**The half the documentation never stated is now settled by running it: the command-line engine option never reaches the scene behind the compositor**, in any version. It writes the engine onto the **active scene alone**, in its place in the argument order, and **writes nothing to disk** — reopening the file gives the stored engines back. **No command-line argument sets a sample count at all**; one passed after the separator is taken and silently ignored at exit 0.

**So the direction that costs money does not happen.** A second scene never renders at a count the user typed for the first.

**One real version difference, re-run and confirmed: Blender 4.5 does not honour a view layer's sample override under EEVEE**, where 5.0, 5.1 and 5.2 do. **It shows on the direct render path too, so it is not a compositor difference.**

**What is bounded, and it is the whole of it: every run was on the Creator's own Mac, on Apple silicon.** Nothing was tried on Linux or Windows. Three smaller things stay open and none blocks: what the node's own re-render button renders with, unsettled in all four versions and wanting a machine with a display server; what a sample count is for Workbench, which is a question about what a document names rather than one a run would fix; and which 5.2 build the topic's earlier questions were answered against, the Mac having moved from 5.2.0 to 5.2.1 between them.

**What it unblocks: Row 492**, which was the last row in this stage waiting on anything.

*(The original entry's remainder follows, unchanged, since what it says breaks is what the answer settled.)*

**What breaks without it, and it is the direction that costs money:** the page states a cost in the artist's own terms **before they pay for it** — *"rendering this scene renders Exterior too, at its own engine and sample count"*. If a typed count or a picked engine does reach the pulled-in scene, that line is wrong, and a second scene renders at a count the user typed for the first.

**What holds without it:** *which* scene pulls in which, which is answered — `Render Settings` §1.1 Q2 item 3, with the reading carried at `Job files and outputs` §4.2 item 3.

**What rests on it:** Row 492.

### Fact 136 — Whether `system-ui` and `ui-monospace` resolve a face with a true italic on each of the three system web views, what each engine does where the resolved face has none, and whether that outcome is distinguishable from upright at the smallest type steps `Preparation/Design/Design values.md` uses.

**Named by:** `Audits/Architecture/Sweep/Design — Design values — Design close re-sweep.md`, 2026-08-25, out of **Row 483**.

**Why it became load-bearing on the day the Design stage closed.** Before the close, *a value Smoozy cannot confirm* was a colour in that document's meaning table. The close took the colour away and put the weight on the face: *"A value Smoozy cannot confirm is shown by the italic face and the dash, not by a colour of its own. Nothing is reserved for it."* **By that sentence's own words the italic is the whole of the distinction.** Neither face is shipped — `Desktop app and delivery` **H25**: *"Neither face is shipped and neither is named per platform. Both are asked for by generic family"*, and *"No platform's face name has to be known by this project, and none is written down anywhere."* — so which italic the user sees is whatever each of the three engines resolves out of `system-ui`, and where the resolved face carries no italic of its own, whatever that engine does instead.

**Three other Design documents already rest on the italic being an unmistakable second state of the same face**, including `Preparation/Design/Whole app.md`'s italic-and-bold convention (Row 316), which is answered at `Preparation/Architecture/Render Settings.md` **J1** — and J1 hands the face back to Design in as many words: *"Which face each takes is Design's; that they are two objects is this clause."* **So the two-object rule is answered and the face is not.**

**Not already answered, and the sweep says where it looked.** The whole of `Preparation/Architecture/` searched for *italic*, *oblique*, *font-style*, *synthes* and *font-weight*: **the only occurrence of *italic* anywhere in the stage is J1**, which assigns the face to Design and answers nothing about it. §2.7's H25 to H29 answer the family, the fallback chain, the unrecognised-generic case and the fixed-width column, and **none of the five mentions style at all**. **Fact 119 is the nearest entry in this register and is about which family resolves, not about style.**

**Answered by:** reading the standard's and the three engine projects' own publications — the same route Facts 117, 118 and 119 were answered by. **No rental.**

**Machine:** none. By `Desktop app and delivery` **Y3** no Windows machine and no Linux desktop computer has run it, so the per-engine half **closes as documentation alone** — by necessity for the Linux desktop third, and because no Windows machine was rented for the Windows third — exactly as Rows 321, 322 and 327 did.

**Shape-deciding:** no. **Posable:** yes, as documentation, now.

**ANSWERED in part, 2026-08-25 — settled on the Mac, unanswerable on Linux by construction, and open on Windows.** See `The three desktop systems read again — dying, revealing, resolving, and what the shell does not expose.md` §5. **The Mac is observed and good**: both faces ship genuine designed italics, proved by turning synthesis off and watching them not move while two control faces collapsed exactly to upright. **Linux has no answer to give** — the face is whatever the desktop is configured to use. **Windows is open, and its citation check narrowed the question**: the stated Windows 11 interface face ships no italic at all, and what the web view resolves to is that system's **menu font**, which is answerable without a Windows computer. **The earlier account of that gap rested on a code excerpt that does not exist in that form**, welded from two places; the conclusion moved and the ground under it was replaced.

**One note from the sweep the runner should have.** It is narrow enough that answering it inside `Desktop app and delivery` **2.7 The two faces**, beside H25 to H29, would close it; and the sweep observed that it might have gone at Row 316's number rather than a new one, Row 316 being the italic convention's own home. **The sweep asserted only that it is not answered today and that nothing in the trail has ever been asked it.** The numbering is the runner's and is now made.

**What rests on it:** Row 483, `Preparation/Design/Design values.md`'s meaning table as the close rewrote it, and through Row 316 the italic-and-bold convention every settings page uses.

### Fact 137 — Each platform's own keyboard-shortcut conventions for the menu items Smoozy carries, which combinations each system reserves, and whether the chosen shell exposes an accelerator beside a menu item — on a platform's native menu bar and on the application-drawn one-button arrangement Windows and Linux use.

**Named by:** `Audits/Architecture/Sweep/Design — the mockup — Design close re-sweep.md`, 2026-08-25, out of **Row 474**. **Thirteen key combinations are drawn** in the mockup's menu bar, every one of them in macOS notation, on an application `Preparation/Architecture/Desktop app and delivery.md` builds for three systems.

**Rule 2 forbids answering it from memory, and the sweep says so in as many words.** The precedent is exact: `Preparation/Design/Whole app.md` labelled its menu-**bar** sentence an assumption and handed it to this stage, which answered it at `Desktop app and delivery` §18.1 K1 to K3 from each platform's own documentation. **The accelerators went with nothing.**

**Not answered anywhere, and the sweep names where it looked.** §18 in full — K5 fixes the five menus and says *"This document builds the integration and adds no item"*, K6 classifies Window and Help, K7 routes cut, copy and paste to whatever has focus, K8 sends menu item labels through the typography point; **nothing about an accelerator, a modifier or a per-system mapping.** §21's service table, whose menus entry reads *"the five menus' integration per platform, and one picker service"* — a key beside an item is not in it, and A1 forbids a module reaching the operating system by any other route. **Every file in `Preparation/Architecture/`**, searched for *keyboard shortcut*, *accelerator*, *mnemonic*, *modifier key*, *key combination*, *Command key*, *Control key* and the `⌘` character itself: **no hits of any kind.** `Preparation/Specs/Whole app.md`, the same searches: none — **the specs promise no shortcut anywhere.** **This register neither asks for it nor answers it.**

**Answered by:** reading each platform's own published conventions and the chosen shell's own documentation. **No rental and no machine.**

**Machine:** none. The same three-system documentation-only shape as Fact 129, and here it is documentation-only for two of the three: no Windows machine and no Linux desktop computer has run it to press a key on. **By necessity for the Linux desktop third; for the Windows third because none was rented**, a Windows machine being rentable.

**Shape-deciding:** no, and the sweep bounds what is at stake rather than leaving it open. **K10's shape applies**: a shell that could not carry an accelerator would cost the key and not a promise, because every act with a key drawn on it has a control of its own elsewhere in the app. **So it is a property of the route rather than an escalation, exactly as the menu bar itself is. What it is not is answered.**

**Posable:** yes, as documentation, now.

**ANSWERED, 2026-08-25, on all three.** See `The three desktop systems read again — dying, revealing, resolving, and what the shell does not expose.md` §6. **The load-bearing half: the chosen shell's accelerator works only with a platform-native menu bar**, and the shell publishes no free-standing shortcut facility — so on Windows and Linux, where Smoozy draws its own menus in the web view, the keystroke is the page's to catch. **Linux publishes no conventions list at all** beyond two drafts and one rule. **Windows publishes more than the reading found** — its own reference carries F1 for Help and three further menu conventions the reading missed, having hit a forum, correctly refused it, and then stopped rather than asking where else the same vendor publishes.

**What breaks without it:** the combinations are promised in the drawing and in no other document in the project, so **what a Windows or a Linux user presses is written down nowhere at all**, and a combination a system reserves for itself is drawn on a menu that cannot honour it.

**What rests on it:** Row 474, and §18's menu integration where an accelerator would have to be declared.

### Fact 138 — How a notification activation reaches an unpackaged, unelevated desktop binary on each of macOS, Windows and Linux, and whether the chosen shell exposes it.

**Named by:** `Audits/Architecture/Sweep/Design — Whole app — Design close re-sweep.md`, 2026-08-25, out of **Row 480** — *"Clicking brings Smoozy forward and goes to the thing it is about"*, labelled **settled at Design** (2026-08-23).

**The architecture is written the other way, which is what makes it a fact rather than a detail.** `Preparation/Architecture/Desktop app and delivery.md` Section 24 hands Design *"what clicking one does"* — the wording and the destination — and **does not establish that a click arrives at all.** Section 13 says the opposite: **T22** *"Nothing in the application is advanced, cleared, acknowledged or recorded by a notification being posted"* and **T23** *"A post is therefore attempted and forgotten, and no state depends on the attempt's result."* **An attempt that is forgotten holds no subject to route back to.**

**The bar is set by Section 13's own findings, which are all preconditions of the same kind:** macOS needs a bundle identity to post at all (T2); Windows needs a Start-menu shortcut carrying an application identifier and **posts fail silently** (T3, T4); Linux's own specification says clients *"must not assume that it is available"* (T5). **Linux actions are a further question of the same shape** — Section 13 already records that the picture has no capability string, so a client cannot ask in advance whether the server will honour it.

**Fact 73 answered the posting preconditions and stops there.** This register holds no entry for activation; the sweep searched it for *activation* and *clicked* and found nothing.

**Answered by:** reading each system's own documentation and the chosen shell's. **No rental and no machine.**

**Machine:** none. The same three-system documentation-only shape, with the Windows and Linux thirds so by necessity.

**Shape-deciding:** no. **Posable:** yes, as documentation, now.

**ANSWERED in part, 2026-08-25 — the running-application case on all three, the cold-start case open on two.** See `The three desktop systems read again — dying, revealing, resolving, and what the shell does not expose.md` §7. **A click on a notification reaches a running Smoozy on all three systems, and the shell ships that in its current released version** rather than only in its unreleased next one. **What is unproven is a click arriving after Smoozy has quit** — the shell's Windows path parses no command line, and the Linux specification is silent on a server whose posting application has gone. **One negative is bounded rather than flat:** the way the shell registers on Windows is documented by nobody **today**, a relevant vendor page having been retired, and its contents were not inferred from a search snippet.

**What breaks without it, and the sweep is careful about whose promise it is:** **it is not a promise the spec makes** — the spec says nothing about clicking. It is a promise `Preparation/Design/Whole app.md` makes, that no notification is a dead end. A negative leaves four notification kinds that say a thing and can only be acted on by the user finding it themselves. **What keeps the destination reachable either way** is T22's other half — *"Every one of the four kinds also leaves its own durable in-app record"* — which is why this is a routing question rather than a storage one.

**What rests on it:** Row 480.

### Fact 139 — What each of the three systems does with an application-supplied Help menu. **STRUCK 2026-08-25, unasked and unpaid for.**

**Named by:** `Audits/Architecture/Sweep/Design — Whole app — Design close re-sweep.md`, 2026-08-25, out of **Row 481**.

**It evaporated exactly as it was filed to.** The fact existed only while *Report a problem…* sat in a Help menu against `Preparation/Specs/Reporting a problem.md`'s Creator-stated *"In the Smoozy menu, always"*. **The Creator ruled for the spec on 2026-08-25**: the item is in the Smoozy menu, the Help menu held nothing else and was removed, and there is no application-supplied Help menu on any of the three systems to ask a question about.

**No publication was read for it and nothing was spent.** Row 481 is struck, `Desktop app and delivery` K5 and K6 are narrowed to four menus and one operating-system menu, and `Reporting a problem` X3 is written to the ruling.

**Machine:** none. **Shape-deciding:** no. **Posable:** no — the subject no longer exists.

**NOT ANSWERED, AND OWED ONLY CONDITIONALLY.**

**What rests on it:** Row 481, and `Desktop app and delivery` K6.

### Fact 140 — Whether two paths naming one folder can be told to be one on each of the three systems, and by what call.

**Named by:** `Audits/Architecture/Sweep/Design — Settings — Design close re-sweep.md`, 2026-08-25, out of **Row 484** — the searched-folder list's rule that a folder already on the list *"is not added twice and is not refused either: the list flashes the row it is already on"*, labelled **settled at Design** (2026-08-23).

**Why byte equality will not do it, and this project established that itself.** Deciding that a path the picker just returned **is** a path already on the list is a comparison, and on the three systems Smoozy runs on **a folding volume, a Windows path with a trailing period or space, a short-name component, and a case-preserving-but-folding directory each make two different strings name one folder.**

**Nothing supplies the rule, and three near neighbours are not it.** `Preparation/Architecture/Dependency Upload & Remap.md` **F1** says only *"Managing it is Design's; the order is the user's"*, and that document's supply clause enumerates what the screen managing the list is given and stops there. **F9's byte equality is the match rule for a file inside a walk and is expressly not a rule about paths** — *"No folding, no normalising and no canonicalising sits anywhere in the match path"* — and **F14a's stored-name readings exist to make the walk exact, not to compare two entries on a settings list.** `Desktop app and delivery` **K13** judges what a picker returns as a name before anything is written; it compares it to nothing. **Fact 16** is about a file the search reports as a match, `Dependency Upload & Remap` §12 item 11 is which filesystem a distribution presents, and item 21 is that the two stored-name readings have never been run: **none of the three is this question.** The stored-name apparatus F14a cites **may well be** the answer; the sweep declined to assert it and so does this entry.

**Answered by:** reading each operating system's own documentation. **No rental and no machine.**

**Machine:** none. The same three-system shape as Fact 129, with the Windows and Linux desktop thirds documentation-only by necessity.

**Shape-deciding:** no. **Posable:** yes, as documentation, now.

**ANSWERED, 2026-08-25, on all three.** See `The three desktop systems read again — dying, revealing, resolving, and what the shell does not expose.md` §8. **Every system can answer it, and on every system the answer is identity numbers rather than canonicalised text** — a hard link alone gives two different canonical spellings for one object. **The finding that will bite:** the Mac's own file-identity value **disagrees with the low-level status call through a symbolic link**, because the lookup does not follow a trailing link and so identifies the link rather than its target; resolving links first makes them agree. Windows publishes the identical trap. **And no vendor publishes identity across network filesystems**, so the answer is a local one. Windows and Linux are documentation alone.

**What breaks without it:** two rows naming one folder sit on a list whose whole purpose is an order — *"the first folder holding a match wins"* — so a duplicate the comparison missed makes the user's own ordering describe a list that has two entries where the disk has one, **and the flash the design promises never fires for the user most likely to need it.**

**What holds without it:** the containment half of the same promise — *a folder inside one already on the list is added* — which is delivered by construction at `Dependency Upload & Remap` F2, F3 and F4, and is not part of this fact.

**What rests on it:** Row 484.

### Fact 141 — Whether a render time limit is counted the same way on the graphics cards as it is on the processor.

**Numbered by an implementer under the architecture runner on 2026-09-02**, at the runner's instruction. **Until that date this question had no fact, no row and no owner.** `Preparation/Specs/Render Settings.md` kept it as a marker of its own, and **Row 150 disowned it** — *"the spec keeps its own marker for the time-limit half of that, which is Blender Research's rather than this stage's"* — routing it to a stage that is now closed. An unnumbered question is invisible to the count and no row can wait on it, which is the same ground Facts 84, 90, 95 and 96 were numbered on.

**Named by:** no sweep. It comes from the one condition every run behind the answered half shares.

**Why it is not already covered.** *Settings 2* answers the limit's behaviour in all four versions — twenty-four runs of a three-frame render under a ten-second limit, three per version on the Creator's Mac and three per version on an idle rented Linux machine, **every one writing all three frames and taking about three times the limit** — so **a limit is spent on each frame rather than across the whole job**. **Every one of those runs was on the processor.** The project's own comparison lists *whether the Time Limit is counted the same way on a graphics card* among what it did **not** establish (`Preparation/Blender Research/Comparison/Settings.md`, *What is not established*), so there is nothing on the research side to carry across either.

**Answered by:** a rented machine with a working graphics card and a Blender on it, running the same fixture under the same limit on the cards and counting the frames written.
**Machine:** a rental with a card. **Shape-deciding:** no. **Posable:** not by this stage.

**PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02. This is not a pending item and must not be read as one.** It wants a rental with a card, and the Creator ruled on **2026-08-31** that there will be no further rental to answer a question; the research route is closed by the same day's ruling against further Blender research. **No reader may leave a space in the code or the record for the answer.**

**Breaks, and it reaches the user directly — this is the sharpest thing about the entry.** `Preparation/Specs/Render Settings.md` tells the user a limit is spent on each frame and that a long animation costs the limit many times over. **If the cards count it across the whole job instead, that sentence is false on exactly the machines Smoozy rents**: the render stops after one limit's worth in all, **fewer frames come back than were paid for**, and nothing says why. **So the settings page states a cost that is not true**, which is a statement about what the user gets rather than about a mechanism.

**What holds.** The exposure is narrower than the sentence. `Render Settings` **C1** switches the time limit off as part of the imposition set it states against the job's scene, so **a job the user typed a sample count for meets no time limit at all, on either device**; what is exposed is a file carrying its own limit that Smoozy leaves alone. And the answered half stands as observed — per frame, on the processor, in all four versions.

**The silence is removed by a statement that already reaches the user, and no second channel is opened.** A job that ends early leaves frames absent, and `Job files and outputs` **C27**'s completeness statement already reports every frame it expected and did not find, readable afterwards with nobody connected. **So fewer frames than planned is stated; the reason is not claimed** — nothing here asserts why a job stopped, which is what this fact can no longer be made to say.

**What rests on it:** `Preparation/Specs/Render Settings.md`'s time-limit paragraph, whose marker was cleared on 2026-09-02 against this permanence, and **Row 150**, which named the question and gave it away.

- **Corrected 2026-08-22 by the multi-provider batch: two of those six are answered and the bullet above was stale within hours of being relied on.** **The multi-card host is done, twice over** — two hosts of four cards each, rented as two-card slices, on which `nvidia-smi` reported the slice honestly while `/proc/driver/nvidia/gpus` reported all four of the host's cards including two UUIDs belonging to another tenant. **The arithmetic consequence is no longer unobserved: it is a factor of two on both boxes, and `nproc` and memory were the whole host's.** **Fact 28's more-than-one-physical-disk clause is done too** — Sesterce delivers `vda` (97 GB) and `vdb` (787 GB) as standard, two separate virtio devices rather than two partitions of one. **What still genuinely wants hardware is four rather than six:** a box with **no card at all** and not from Vast (Fact 46's second half), a **card make other than NVIDIA** (Fact 3), a **Secure Boot** machine (Fact 81), and Fact 78's **rollback boundary**.
- **The verification round of 2026-08-20 closed more of this register than any rental has, and it rented nothing and spent nothing.** Applied over three sittings ending 2026-08-21. **Answered or reframed here:** Fact 7's stat half, Fact 11's addendum, Fact 21's route, Fact 22, Fact 28's volume sentence, Fact 30's granularity halves and its Windows earliness, Fact 35 half B, Fact 39's Windows **and** Linux clauses, Fact 42, Fact 55's stale reading, Fact 59's Windows half, Fact 63, Fact 65, Fact 66, Fact 76's two open clauses, Fact 79's Windows documentation route, Fact 82, Fact 86, Fact 92's two tightenings, Fact 93's false silence and false *on no rented machine*, Fact 96, Fact 107 re-posed, and Fact 112 closed. **The pattern behind almost all of it is one sentence:** a manual page is not the boundary of a project's documentation — release notes, protocol and format files, published package manifests, a vendor's own reference implementation and headers shipped in the operating system are the same authority — which is now `Method.md`'s standing note. **The second pattern is cheaper still and has no cure but attention:** several of these were answered days earlier, by this project, and the register was not brought forward.

- **Two facts were added on 2026-08-21 out of Row 406, both answered the day they were numbered and neither wanting a machine.** **Fact 121** — Windows *does* document a call returning a file's on-disk capitalisation, in its own published protocol specification, bounded to NTFS and ReFS. **Fact 122** — an ext4 case-folding directory stores the name it was given, which settles a contradiction this project recorded against itself. **Both came out of `Method`'s standing note that a manual page is not the boundary of a project's documentation**, and **Fact 16's Windows half is struck through as stale in consequence** — the thirteen pages that closed it were manual and reference pages, and the Win32 enumeration carrying the class describes it nowhere. **This is the fourth and fifth fact that note has closed.**

- **Fact 123 was added on 2026-08-21 out of Row 425**, answered the same day from the shell's own documentation and its own release notes, wanting no machine — **whether the application can give up the native window frame and draw the title bar itself on all three systems.** It is the first entry in this register about the window frame at all; the sweep that named it recorded that its nearest numbered neighbours are about menu bars, download folders and update replacement. **The release notes are what made it per-system evidence rather than an assumption from a flag with no platform table.**

- **Three facts were added on 2026-08-22 out of the third sanity sweep's verification round, all three answered the day they were numbered and none wanting a machine.** **Fact 124** — installing a distribution package is a privileged operation in the mechanism's own words, which made `Desktop app and delivery` T4's *no route asks for administrator* false and produced a Creator ruling the same day. **Fact 125** — the file-transfer subsystem's exclusive create is an atomic at-most-one claim available to a caller with no program on the machine, which is what `Rented-server runtime` G1b uses where no kernel lock can be held. **Fact 126** — a subsystem handle does not survive its channel, which is what the cap-of-one lease change costs. **Two of the three were run rather than read**, free, on the Creator's own Mac, each with a control either side.

- **The count of facts is derived rather than remembered, and it is stated once, at the top of this file** — re-derived there from the `### Fact N` headings on 2026-08-22. *(This bullet said *The count of facts is 126 … re-checked by script on 2026-08-21 after Fact 123 was filed: 123 headed entries, numbered 1 to 123 with no gap and no duplicate*, which states two different numbers in one sentence; corrected 2026-08-22.)* **Facts 116 to 120 were all numbered on 2026-08-21** — 116 unanswered and riding on Facts 99 and 100's rental, and 117 to 120 answered the same day from documentation and wanting no machine at all. **Fifteen carry an explicit *not answered* marker** — 14, 64, 69, 97, 99, 100, 101, 102, 103, 104, 105, 106, 107, 109 and 110 — of which 104 is a Blender question that belongs to Blender Research rather than here, and 107 is re-posed on a narrower question than it was numbered for. **The *wants a rented machine* figure below is the one number in this file that is still carried rather than derived**, and it has been counted three different ways in two files; whoever next settles it should read the `Machine:` line of each of the fifteen rather than adjust the total.

- **Twelve facts were added on 2026-08-25 out of the ninth sitting's fifteen sweeps — Facts 129 to 140 — and every one of them arrives unanswered.** **None wants a rented machine of its own**, and only **131** touches a machine at all: the Blender crash file's environment values, which rides free on any rental taken for another batch, because the crash file's trigger, its location, its three sections and the fact that an out-of-memory kill writes none at all are **already settled by the research** and must not be paid for twice. **No batch was created and none was changed.** **Ten close on documentation alone**, and for **129, 130, 132, 136, 137, 138, 139 and 140 that is by necessity for the Linux desktop third and for want of a rental for the Windows third**: they want a computer of each of the three systems Smoozy runs on, and `Desktop app and delivery` **Y3** records that no Windows machine and no Linux desktop computer has run it, so the Windows and Linux thirds are documentation alone exactly as Rows 321, 322 and 327 already closed. **Two are Blender's — 134 and 135** — numbered here only so their rows have a name, in the same position as Facts 104, 127 and 128; **both were looked up rather than assumed**, by the fixed route, and `The map.md` poses no question for either while the joined answers stop one step short, so whoever opens Blender Research next should treat them as new questions rather than unlooked-up ones. **132 is the one to answer first**, owed in four places at once with one answer closing all four, and `Download` H7 has carried its gap since it was written. **139 evaporated, on 2026-08-25**, the Creator having ruled *Report a problem…* into the Smoozy menu where its spec always put it; the Help menu went with it and the fact was struck unasked.
